# Prompt quality of the nine plugins — what a calibrated review measured

Run `2026-09-08T17:57:42Z` · rubric `6a92f9a52857` · generated by what is now `plugins/nacharbeit/scripts/build_report.py` from `analysis/nacharbeit/` (do not edit by hand).

Every judgement finding below was produced by a sonnet finder that first had to pass a planted-defect calibration, then survived an adversarial sonnet refuter that re-opened the file and searched for the quote. Mechanical findings come from what is now `plugins/nacharbeit/scripts/nacharbeit_lint.py` (25 regex/YAML rules at the time of this run, each sabotage-tested). The rule ids resolve in the nacharbeit rubric, [`plugins/nacharbeit/references/rubric.md`](plugins/references/rubric).

## How to read this report

Start with section 1. It says how much to trust everything below it: the finder's recall on planted defects, the sealed hold-out it never trained on, and how accurately the router proxy reproduced the known-answer prompts.

Section 2 is the verdict per plugin, one row each, written by opus from the verified findings. Read the summary cell before the numbers.

Section 3 is cannibalization. Every pair there was picked together by a haiku router for the same user prompt, then judged. A pair marked intended-handoff needs no change.

Section 4 lists every verified judgement finding by severity, with the quote the finder cited and the fix the impact lens approved or revised. A warning sign on a fix means applying it as written would make something else worse.

Section 5 is the mechanical lint, grouped by rule. These are the findings a script can prove and a script can fix.

Sections 6 and 7 are the backlog by model tier and the cross-plugin synthesis. The ten actions at the end of section 7 are the ordered starting list; the appendix shows which batch and angle each finding came from, so silence in a cell can be read for what it is.

## 1. The instrument, before the verdict

| measure | value | meaning |
|---|---|---|
| calibration round 1 | recall 0.76, weakest angle 0.40, 0 false positive(s) on clean files | target recall ≥ 0.8, every angle ≥ 0.6 |
| calibration round 2 | recall 0.80, weakest angle 0.60, 1 false positive(s) on clean files | target recall ≥ 0.8, every angle ≥ 0.6 |
| **sealed hold-out recall** | **0.79** | measured once on fixtures the rewriter never saw; floor 0.7 |
| raw → verified findings | 464 → 216 (+28 from the critic round) | the refuter's kill rate |
| router proxy accuracy | 0.97 top-1 on 70 known-answer prompts (top-3 68) | router proxy accurate; collisions are measured |

The finder prompt was rewritten 1 time(s) during calibration:

- after round 1: Restructured the prompt from one flat paragraph of angle hints into an explicit order of operations, the eight (sharpened) rules of engagement, per-angle reading strategies, and a closing self-check, because every missed class traced to a check the reviewer never performed rather than a judgement it got wrong. The single largest addition is a mandatory frontmatter-key pass: the old prompt implicitly treated `description` as the only frontmatter of interest, so obligations carried by load/scope/execution keys were invisible — the new step 2 makes every key in scope and pairs it with a procedure-angle rule mapping a forking execution context to the requirement that the body contain an actionable task. Four further generalisations target the other misses without inviting noise, each phrased as a discriminating test rather than a keyword list: step-logic now defines steps and loops semantically (prose "then … then" chains count; "keep going / after every X / until nothing new" are loops) and states that convergence-only stop conditions still fail absent a bound and an escalation path; clarity gets a delete-test for paragraphs that define things a competent model already knows, plus a sharpened example criterion that keeps the rubric's template mis-flag intact by making the finding turn on whether a placeholder names a vocabulary the file never enumerates and whether a filled instance and the empty branch are shown; procedure now states that a description built from quoted user phrasings satisfies only the "when" half and overfits literal queries (with "report it once" to prevent it double-firing across rules), and adds a progressive-disclosure test asking whether body-resident bulk is really the standing instruction, especially when a reference file already covers the same material. Since the calibration set produced no false positives, no rule needed a new negative criterion, so precision is protected instead by generic guards — quote-before-claim ordering, one defect one rule, the standing known-mis-flag exclusion, and an explicit "never manufacture a finding to fill an angle" — and by phrasing each new cue as a condition that clean files fail to meet. Length is 799 words.

Sealed defects the frozen finder still missed (the report's blind spots): `test/plugins/fixtures/nacharbeit/sealed-2/skills/quill-lint/SKILL.md Q-OTHER-RIGIDITY`, `test/plugins/fixtures/nacharbeit/sealed-2/skills/quill-lint/SKILL.md Q-OTHER-SCRIPT-INTENT`, `test/plugins/fixtures/nacharbeit/sealed-2/skills/prose-check/SKILL.md Q-CANN-INTERNAL`.

| **andon** | needs-work | 21 | 10 | Six majors, all local fixes; nothing needs a component redesigned or merged. The three most consequential: (1) plugins/andon/references/okf-ledger-schema.md carries two false contracts — it places ANDON_BOARD.md in output_dir when andon-status only ever writes &lt;ledger_dir&gt;/ANDON_BOARD.html, and it claims tags derive from first-class fields while `lane:fast\|slow` has no documented source (it is evidence-doc-only in andon_core.py). (2) plugins/andon/skills/andon-preflight/SKILL.md promises no writes beyond a ledger-writability test, then Step 3 writes PREFLIGHT.md and preflight_summary.json — and only the markdown write is gated by validate-write-path, with no stated behaviour on failure. (3) plugins/andon/skills/andon-loop/SKILL.md advertises a stage-or-gap filter argument no phase reads, and Phase 6's check-convergence call consumes a gaps-closed tally and a wire-status array that no earlier phase is told to build. Also major: andon-adjudicator.md's only non-overridable stop depends on Tier 1 structural evidence none of its three named feeders produce. Done well: routing is clean — every measured collision is an intended handoff, an unresolvable bare-status/preflight prompt, or (andon-preflight vs self-assess-preflight) a defect on the other side. |
| **cli-scaffold** | needs-work | 19 | 3 | Three problems dominate. (1) `agents/cli-scaffold-verifier.md` is the weakest component: its description is 1361 chars against the 1024 cap and embeds `<example>` XML tags, and its body leaves three control-flow holes — exit code 2 is listed but never handled, the MAX_FIX_ITERATIONS HALT is never reported to the caller, and step 3 reads a report JSON whose path is only documented for the exit-1 branch. (2) All three paradigm skills (`skills/cli-scaffold-{compiled,interpreted,shell}/SKILL.md`) treat "the resolved language/dialect" as given while their own descriptions invite direct invocation, with no stated precondition or refusal path. (3) `skills/scaffold-cli/SKILL.md` step 1 loops AMBIGUOUS back into itself with no retry cap or escalation. Correction to one finding: `skills/cli-architecture/SKILL.md`'s "12" is not an off-by-one — `scripts/constants.py` asserts `LANGUAGE_COUNT = 12` and comments that POSIX sh "does not add to LANGUAGE_COUNT"; renumbering to 13 would contradict enforced code, so the fix is wording, not arithmetic. Done well: language resolution is delegated to `scripts/lang_router.py` rather than guessed, and doctrine lives in one skill with an explicit no-duplication rule. |
| **codebase-consistency** | needs-work | 33 | 1 | Fifteen majors, all local fixes; no component needs redesign, and one item needs a human decision rather than a rewrite. (2) commands/consistency-align.md is the weakest command file: its no-approved-brief gate names no remedial command, the pilot has no branch for failing tests before PLAYBOOK.md is written and the fan-out begins, the required per-unit `deps` has no stated source, and `[dimension]` is never bound to `$2`. (3) commands/consistency-verify.md passes no module list to a workflow described as fanning out per aligned module, and never says to read ALIGN_NOTES.md. Done well: where gates exist they are specific and enforceable — align-executor.md's missing-PLAYBOOK STOP, align.md's 2/3 circuit breaker, verify.md's two-cause blocker — and consistency-scan.md's description is already well-scoped with explicit exclusions. |
| **compass** | needs-work | 43 | 2 | Three problems dominate. (2) plugins/lehre/agents/pattern-investigator.md:22 tells the agent to prefer serena's find_symbol/find_referencing_symbols while frontmatter grants only Read, Glob, Grep, Bash — silent grep fallback in a portable agent. (1) Artifact contracts that no step defines: `skills/self-assess-extract-rules/SKILL.md` resolves output paths for DATA_OBJECTS.md and business_rules.json that nothing in the pipeline produces or validates (human-tier), and `skills/self-assess-stage-map/SKILL.md` calls stage_map.json optional while `skills/self-assess-transform-brief/SKILL.md` Step 7 assumes it exists, with stage_map_summary.json never described at all. (2) Missing absent-input branches in `skills/self-assess-transform-brief/SKILL.md`: arch_health_summary.json and business_rules_summary.json are read as decision sources with no stated behaviour when the producing skill never ran, unlike the explicit stage_graph.json gate. (3) Tool grants that contradict stated roles: five read-only agents (convention-auditor, business-rules-miner, arch-health-auditor, idiom-auditor, ui-auditor) hold Bash they never use, while `agents/transform-executor.md` is told to delete and move files with no tool that can. Separately, all 16 skill descriptions are when-only and carry no negative scope, which is what the measured collisions with cupertino, confab, lehre and andon all trace back to. ## 3. Cannibalization — measured by routing, judged by opus

router proxy accurate; collisions are measured. Pairs below were picked together by a haiku router for the ambiguous prompts in what is now `plugins/nacharbeit/scripts/ambiguous-prompts.json`.

| pair | prompts | verdict | winner | rationale | tier |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `cupertino-handbook-check` ↔ `self-assess-lint-audit` | 2 | add-negative-trigger | self-assess-lint-audit | No handoff edge connects these two — the graph shows two closed, disjoint loops (cupertino-handbook-draft/check/fix/apply + handbook-drift-auditor; self-assess-autopilot/lint-audit/convention-auditor/idiom-fix), so this is not an intended handoff. Both are legitimate and structurally distinct: reading the SKILL.md bodies confirms the only real discriminator is which written artifact holds the rules. cupertino-handbook-check requires `.cupertino/<domain>-handbook.md` and its Step 1 bails to cupertino-handbook-draft when that file is absent; self-assess-lint-audit reads `.claude/house-rules.md` with a labelled CLAUDE.md fallback, caps dispatch at lint_max_rules, and writes LINT_AUDIT.md for autopilot's CHECK phase. Different inputs, different outputs, different pipelines — so not a merge, and not a rescope either, since each description already names its own source document. The collision is purely in the generic quoted triggers: cupertino literally claims "check this against our standards" while self-assess claims "check our conventions", and neither disclaims the other, so an artifact-blind prompt hits both. Fix is to disambiguate by artifact rather than by phrasing. self-assess-lint-audit wins these two prompts: "our standards" and "the conventions we wrote down" name no handbook, and CLAUDE.md/house-rules.md is the artifact that exists in essentially every repo, whereas a cupertino handbook only exists after a deliberate draft pass. Tier is sonnet rather than haiku because cupertino's trigger list is edited (the ambiguous "check this against our standards" phrase is removed, not just supplemented), not merely appended to. No pipeline order changes. | sonnet |
| `cupertino-handbook-draft` ↔ `lehre-codify` | 2 | add-negative-trigger |  | No handoff edge connects the two (cupertino-handbook-draft's neighbours are all cupertino: handbook-apply/check and handbook-dimension-analyst; lehre-codify's are all lehre: preflight/decompose/gauge/conform plus its three researcher agents), so this is not an intended handoff. Nor is it a merge: the two do genuinely different jobs on different axes. cupertino-handbook-draft is descriptive and domain-scoped — pick exactly one of code/design/testing/documentation, fan out over a fixed 6-dimension catalog, and write a human-readable `.cupertino/<domain>-handbook.md` recording what the project already does (or honestly labelling a dimension `scaffolded` when it has no convention). lehre-codify is prescriptive and pipeline-bound — it runs after lehre-preflight/lehre-decompose, dispatches doctrine-researcher per rule-domain, requires every rule to cite an external authority, assigns severity by whether a deterministic predicate exists, and emits `.lehre/ruleset.json` for lehre-gauge/violation-auditor to enforce. Neither should absorb the other, and neither needs narrowing: each already owns its territory in its body text.  The collision is purely lexical in the descriptions. Both surface as "project conventions written down as rules", and neither says which side of descriptive-vs-prescriptive it sits on, nor that one produces a prose handbook while the other produces a machine-enforced JSON ruleset inside a multi-step pipeline. The two colliding prompts actually have different correct answers, which is why a single winner is not named: "capture how this project actually does things, one rule per dimension" is cupertino-handbook-draft verbatim (actual conventions, per-dimension), while "what conventions should this project follow? write them down as rules" is lehre-codify — "should follow" is the authority-research framing, and lehre-codify's description already lists the near-identical trigger "what rules should this project follow". A router that reads both descriptions today has nothing to separate them on; adding a mutual "not for X — use Y" clause supplies exactly that discriminator without touching either pipeline. Pure clause insertion, existing text unchanged, hence haiku. | haiku |
| `confab-contract-drift` ↔ `self-assess-docs-drift` | 2 | add-negative-trigger | self-assess-docs-drift | The two skills have genuinely disjoint territory and no handoff edge between them: confab-contract-drift's body states "Never extract or verify prose documentation — only structural, machine-checkable declarations", while self-assess-docs-drift reads prose files (CLAUDE.md/README/ARCHITECTURE/DECISIONS/ADR) and verifies falsifiable claims against cited code. So this is neither a merge nor a real scope overlap — the jobs differ, the pipelines differ (contract-auditor + cycle_engine vs docs-drift-auditor + autopilot CHECK), and neither names the other.  The collision is purely lexical, and it lives in the *descriptions*, not the prompts, so "leave" is wrong. Two words do all the damage. (1) "docstrings" in confab's description reads as "docs" to a router; the disambiguating rule that confab means docstring parameter/return declarations and not prose lives only in the SKILL.md body, which the router never sees. (2) "after a refactor" is confab's own trigger phrase and appears verbatim in the colliding prompt, while "verify our docs match the code" is self-assess's near-verbatim trigger — so the second prompt hands each skill half of its own description.  For these prompts the winner is self-assess-docs-drift: "the docs still match the code" in ordinary usage means documentation files, and the refactor clause is circumstance, not scope. "check for drift" alone is genuinely underdetermined — but the fix is still to make each description say what it is *not*, so that a bare "drift" ask surfaces the choice rather than silently picking the wrong auditor.  Both proposed rewrites are pure negative-trigger insertions that preserve every existing trigger phrase and change no pipeline ordering; self-assess-docs-drift's also promotes the ci-topology exclusion that its Step 2 already enforces in code, so the description matches the guard. fix_tier haiku. | haiku |
| `lehre-codify` ↔ `self-assess-extract-rules` | 1 | add-negative-trigger |  | No handoff edge connects these two, and the source files confirm they mine different things. lehre-codify is prescriptive: it researches a code style / layering / error-handling / dependency-direction doctrine from external authority (dispatching doctrine-researcher, pattern-investigator, rule-critic) and writes .lehre/ruleset.json with per-rule provenance and severity — rules about HOW code is written. self-assess-extract-rules is descriptive: it dispatches business-rules-miner over executable calculations, validations and state transitions, verifies each file:line citation, and requires a two-judge panel for P0 — rules about WHAT the code decides. Neither is a subset of the other, so merge and rescope are both wrong, and neither pipeline should change (lehre-codify sits between lehre-preflight/decompose and lehre-gauge; extract-rules sits inside autopilot's CHECK phase).  The prompt "extract the rules this codebase follows into something checkable" hits every shared token — extract, rules, this codebase, checkable — and names neither domain. It is under-specified rather than mis-routed, so I name no winner: without knowing whether the user means conventions or domain logic, both are defensible, and a router picking both is arguably the correct behaviour. But the descriptions can still resolve it, because neither currently says what it is NOT. lehre-codify's triggers ("establish our code standards", "codify our architecture") and extract-rules' triggers ("extract business logic", "turn tribal knowledge into a spec") are individually clear yet purely positive; a prompt that matches neither trigger phrase falls back on the generic word "rules", which both own. Adding one explicit exclusion sentence to each — pointing at the other by name, with the how-it-is-written vs what-it-decides distinction spelled out — is enough for a router to either pick correctly on a disambiguated prompt or surface the fork on this one. Worth noting a third neighbour: undocumented-but-observed conventions are codebase-consistency's territory, not lehre-codify's, since lehre-codify refuses any rule without an external authority; that boundary is intact and untouched by this fix. | haiku |
| `consistency-map` ↔ `consistency-scan` | 1 | add-negative-trigger | consistency-scan | These are consecutive stages of one pipeline, not rivals: `/consistency-map`'s body opens by rendering `analysis/$1/consistency.json` "(from `/consistency-scan`)" and writes `matrix.json` from it, so map is strictly downstream and produces nothing without a prior scan (the bidirectional edges in the graph are just "map cites its input" plus "scan names its successor"). The router collision is therefore not a genuine territory dispute — it is caused by map's description omitting the one fact that disqualifies it here. "Render the divergence inventory as a module × convention consistency matrix" names the same object scan produces ("divergence inventory") with no statement that the inventory must already exist, so a discovery prompt like "where does this repo do the same thing two different ways?" reads as an equally good match for both. scan's own description is already well-scoped (it carries two explicit exclusions and needs no change). The minimal fix is one precondition clause plus a negative trigger on map only, pointing discovery prompts at scan; no scoping, merging, or pipeline reordering is warranted, since the current order (scan → map → canonize) is correct and load-bearing. fix_tier haiku: a single-field insertion into one command's frontmatter description, no body edits, no wiring changes. | haiku |
| `compass-map-relationships` ↔ `self-assess-stage-map` | 1 | add-negative-trigger | self-assess-stage-map | These are not the same job. `self-assess-stage-map` is a repo-specific artifact producer: it parses the real import/use graph per detected language, clusters files into stages by shallowest package boundary, and writes a `stage_graph.json` that six other self-assess components read (edges to `self-assess-arch-health`, `-complexity-score`, `-transform-brief`, `-autopilot`, `stage-mapper`). `compass-map-relationships` is a domain-agnostic reasoning technique: it extracts relationship triples and traverses them hop by hop, citing a triple index, and produces no artifact anyone downstream consumes (its only edge is back into `compass-solve`). No handoff edge exists between the two, so this is not an intended handoff, and merging would either destroy the reasoning technique's generality or destroy the stage-graph artifact contract.  The collision is real and is the descriptions' fault, not the prompt's: "map the architecture of this repo" is almost verbatim the first trigger phrase `self-assess-stage-map` claims, and it wins outright — the user wants module boundaries derived from source, not a hop-by-hop causal traversal. But `compass-map-relationships` invites the miss with two unqualified graph-shaped phrases, "entity/dependency/causal structure" and "trace the dependency path", neither of which excludes code dependencies. Nothing in its text tells a router that repository import graphs belong elsewhere.  Rescoping is unnecessary — compass's territory is legitimate and already narrow in kind (traversal over an established triple index), just unbounded in domain. The minimal correct fix is a negative trigger on the compass side only, naming `self-assess-stage-map` and the repo-architecture phrasings. `self-assess-stage-map` needs no change: it already owns the colliding phrase explicitly and adding a mirror exclusion would only lengthen a description that is currently winning on the merits.  fix_tier is haiku: this is a pure negative-trigger insertion appended to one description, with the existing positive triggers left word-for-word intact and no pipeline order touched. | haiku |
| `self-assess-arch-health` ↔ `self-assess-stage-map` | 1 | intended-handoff | self-assess-stage-map | The two skills are the two halves of one declared pipeline, and the colliding prompt is literally the two halves joined by "and": "show me the real module boundaries" is a verbatim trigger phrase in self-assess-stage-map's description, and "where they're violated" is self-assess-arch-health's "layering violations" / cycles / god-modules territory. The dependency is explicit in both directions and in-description: arch-health's description states it "Reads the full stage_graph.json from self-assess-stage-map", and the SKILL.md body (plugins/self-assess/skills/self-assess-arch-health/SKILL.md, Step 0) hard-gates on that file — if stage_graph.json is absent it degrades to "Ready-with-gaps", writes an empty ARCH_HEALTH.md noting that self-assess-stage-map has not run, and stops. Stage-map, symmetrically, "writes the full stage graph other self-assess skills depend on". So a router returning both for this prompt is not confusion — it is the correct pair, in the correct order, and self-assess-autopilot's CHECK phase sequences them exactly this way (stage-map first, arch-health second). Neither description claims the other's territory: stage-map never mentions violations, deficiencies, cycles, or god-modules; arch-health never offers to build or extract the graph. Merging them would fuse a mechanical extraction step that several other consumers (self-assess-complexity-score, self-assess-transform-brief, self-assess-status) read from into a judgement step, and rescoping would only make stage-map refuse a phrase it legitimately owns. The winner for these prompts is self-assess-stage-map — it must fire first because arch-health cannot produce a real answer without its artifact — with arch-health firing immediately after as its declared successor to answer the "and where they're violated" half. No description change is warranted; adding a negative trigger to either would suppress a handoff that is by design. |  |
| `andon-verify` ↔ `self-assess-transform-execute` | 1 | intended-handoff | self-assess-transform-execute | The collision is the wired pipeline, not overlapping territory. `self-assess-transform-execute`'s own description ends with "then hands off to andon-verify without self-verifying", and the SKILL.md backs that with a hard rule (`verify-dispatch-handoff`, Step 6 "Hand off to verification -- never self-verify": it must state its edit is unverified and dispatch `andon:andon-verify`, or `andon-loop` for a ledger-recorded proof, then stop). The handoff graph confirms it with an in-description edge transform-execute -&gt; andon-verify.  The prompt "apply the approved fixes, one at a time, and prove each one" contains both halves of that pipeline verbatim: "apply ... one at a time" is exactly transform-execute's one-authorized-phase-per-invocation contract, and "prove each one" is exactly andon-verify's job (it never applies anything, and its own text forbids writing to the ledger). A router that returns both is returning the correct sequence, in order.  No territory is double-claimed: transform-execute applies and never verifies; andon-verify proves and never edits. Neither description needs narrowing or a negative trigger — adding "not for proving" to the applier would contradict the successor it is required to name. The only real ambiguity is upstream ("the approved fixes" could mean an andon ledger gap or a MODERNIZATION_BRIEF.md phase), which is a prompt-side underspecification between transform-execute and andon-loop, not a defect in this pair. Winner: transform-execute fires first and dispatches andon-verify itself, so ranking both is harmless and ranking andon-verify alone would leave nothing to apply. |  |

There is no handoff edge between them — each only names its own pipeline — so this is not an intended handoff, and merging would fuse two different upstream contracts (rule cards + brief approval vs. modernization findings with severityNote/smell exclusions), two different verifiers, and two plugins' pipeline orders. Rescoping is not needed either: the territories already differ; what collides is the generic surface phrasing both share ("apply exactly this one already-verified mechanical fix at the cited location and nothing else"), which is the whole job description of any per-cluster remediator. The colliding prompt carries no pipeline signal at all — no rule card, no brief, no idiom/modernization vocabulary — so no winner exists for it as written; both agents are dispatch-only leaves that a bare user request should never fire directly. The cheap durable fix is a mutual negative trigger plus an explicit "only ever dispatched by &lt;its orchestrator&gt;" clause, so a prompt with even partial context routes correctly and a context-free one visibly matches neither exclusively. Pure insertion into the descriptions, no body or pipeline change: haiku tier. | haiku |

Known-answer prompts the router got wrong (2):

| prompt | expected | router picked |
|---|---|---|
| scaffold a CLI in some scripting language, whatever's easiest | `consistency-preflight` | `scaffold-cli` |
| where do we violate our own layering, and which of those are real | `self-assess-stage-map` | `self-assess-arch-health` |

## 4. Verified findings

Severity: blocker 0, major 111, minor 131, nit 2. Angle: procedure 64, step-logic 53, meaning 47, clarity 44, contract 27, cannibalization 7, other 2.

Applying that half of the 'or' turns a caller-visible ambiguity (Q-MEANING-CONTRACT) into an active contradiction between documented semantics and actual behavior (Q-MEANING-PROMISE) — the code would promise one thing and silently do the other, which is worse than the original unstated ambiguity. | `plugins/cupertino/workflows/handbook-check.js`:119 | `Q-STEPS-GATE` | A rule whose find-step fails or returns nothing is silently dropped by filter(Boolean) with zero logging, unlike its sibling workflows: handbook-draft.js explicitly logs when a dimension fails to produce a candidate ('dimension(s) failed to produce a candidate and were dropped'), and handbook-fix.js logs skippedCount -- handbook-check.js has no equivalent check comparing perRule.length to rules.length, so an entire rule silently receiving zero coverage looks identical to a rule that was checked and found clean. | ⚠ Per the workflow runtime's pipeline() semantics (documented in workflow-authoring: 'A stage that throws drops that item to null and skips its remaining stages'), pipeline() never shrinks its result array on a failed item -- it nulls that item's slot in place. So perRule.length is always equal to rules.length, whether or not any rule's find-step failed; comparing the two lengths is a no-op that will always report zero dropped rules, which is a guard that looks like it checks something but never can (exactly the failure shape this repo's own CLAUDE.md warns about repeatedly). The correct diagnostic counts null entries in perRule directly: `const droppedRules = perRule.filter((r) => r == null).length; if (droppedRules > 0) log(`${droppedRules} rule(s)' find-step failed and were dropped.`)` placed before the existing `perRule.filter(Boolean).flat().filter(Boolean)` line. | sonnet |

| `plugins/self-assess/skills/self-assess-portfolio/SKILL.md`:28 | `Q-STEPS-INPUTS` | Step 3 checks each swept repo's artifacts against "its configured output_dir," but this skill never calls check-enabled (or any equivalent) per repo to resolve that repo's own .claude/self-assess.local.md output_dir setting, so the value this step reads was never produced by any prior step in this file. | ⚠ Do not use check-enabled for this: self_assess_cli.py's cmd_check_enabled calls settings_mod.require_enabled(), which raises SelfAssessError whenever that repo's .claude/self-assess.local.md sets enabled: false (repo-wide or under the skill's own key) -- see plugins/self-assess/scripts/lib/settings.py's require_enabled(). | `plugins/andon/skills/andon-loop/SKILL.md`:166 | `Q-STEPS-INPUTS` | Phase 4's dispatch to andon-verify never forwards the `verify_strategy`/`verify_strategy_rationale` fields that andon-propose's Phase 1 step 4 was expressly written to produce ('do not leave strategy selection to `andon-verify` to guess; you have the most context on the gap right now'), and andon-verify's own Step 1 unconditionally re-derives the strategy via `route-wire` regardless ('never skip, never default'), so andon-propose's careful strategy choice is recorded on the gap doc but orphaned at the one point it should be consumed. | ⚠ plugins/andon/skills/andon-verify/SKILL.md Step 1 currently reads 'route the wire (never skip, never default)' and the skill's opening line states 'Never default to strategy a (tribunal) as a starting guess -- route through the classifier below first, every time.' | `plugins/cupertino/workflows/handbook-check.js`:69 | `Q-MEANING-ASSUMED` | Input validation only checks that `rules` is a non-empty array; it never checks that each item actually has the `.rule` field that `findPrompt` interpolates (`RULE: ${r.rule}`), so a malformed rules entry silently sends a `RULE: undefined` prompt to the drift-auditor agent instead of failing loudly. | Immediately after the existing `if (!Array.isArray(rules) \|\| rules.length === 0)` guard, add a loop that checks each entry has a non-empty string `.rule` (e.g. `typeof r.rule !== 'string' \|\| !r.rule.trim()`), and throw `cupertino-handbook-check: rules[<i>] is missing a non-empty 'rule' field` naming the first offending index -- matching the existing throw style/message prefix in this file so the caller fails loudly instead of shipping 'RULE: undefined' into the drift-auditor prompt. | sonnet |
| `plugins/cupertino/workflows/handbook-fix.js`:117 | `Q-STEPS-INPUTS` | The Verify phase dispatches verifyFixPrompt for every finding in `c.items` (the pre-remediation cluster) without cross-referencing which of those locations the remediator's own `remediation.results` marked 'applied' vs 'blocked', so a location the remediator explicitly declined to touch is verified for compliance exactly as if a fix had been attempted, conflating 'never attempted' with 'attempted and still wrong'. | ⚠ The component's own description promises verification 'blind to the remediator' -- the current code deliberately re-checks the actual file state for every cluster item regardless of what the remediator self-reported, which is exactly what catches a remediator that mislabels a location (e.g. claims 'blocked' but touched the file anyway, or claims 'applied' incorrectly). Filtering verification to only remediation.results-status==='applied' items, as suggested, makes verification coverage depend on the remediator's own self-report -- the opposite of 'blind' -- and creates a blind spot precisely where a wrong self-report matters most. Keep verifying every item in c.items unconditionally, but attach the matching remediation.results entry's status ('applied'/'blocked'/'missing') to each verification result before returning, so downstream reporting can distinguish 'attempted and still non-compliant' from 'never attempted and still non-compliant' without dropping any independent check. (conflicts with `Q-MEANING-PROMISE`) | sonnet |

Only if the author deliberately wants serena granted should the fix name the exact tool identifiers to add, not "the serena MCP tool." (conflicts with `M-AGENT-TOOLS-SHAPE`) | sonnet |

| `plugins/lehre/agents/pattern-investigator.md`:24 | `Q-PROC-CLAUDEMD-DUP` | The 'burned repeatedly by guards that exist and are never called' rationale for preferring find_referencing_symbols over grep is copied almost word-for-word from the root CLAUDE.md, which already loads automatically. | ⚠ Don't point the reader at 'CLAUDE.md' as the suggested fix does ('see CLAUDE.md on find_referencing_symbols'): pattern-investigator is a lehre-plugin agent dispatched against arbitrary brownfield codebases the user is documenting conventions for, not only against werkstoff itself. A pointer to 'CLAUDE.md' assumes the *target* repo carries this exact rationale in its own CLAUDE.md, which is a hidden, unstated precondition that will usually be false. Also don't delete the paragraph outright -- some standalone justification for preferring find_referencing_symbols over grep is needed precisely because most target repos won't have werkstoff's CLAUDE.md to fall back on. Instead compress it to a short, self-contained line that drops werkstoff's specific incident framing ('burned repeatedly') without citing an external file: e.g. 'grep for a name finds prose mentions; find_referencing_symbols finds actual call sites -- treat them as different signals.' (conflicts with `Q-MEANING-ASSUMED`) | sonnet |

## 7. Cross-plugin synthesis

### Cross-plugin collisions

The router proxy scored **0.971 accuracy on 70 known-answer prompts (68/70 in top-3)**, and the run's own note reads `router proxy accurate; collisions are measured`. These are measured collisions, not hints — each row below is a pair the proxy actually surfaced together, judged against the two components' source files and the handoff graph.

| Components | Verdict | Winner | Rationale |
|---|---|---|---|
| `cupertino-handbook-check` / `self-assess-lint-audit` | add-negative-trigger | self-assess-lint-audit | Disjoint loops; only discriminator is the artifact (`.cupertino/<domain>-handbook.md` vs `.claude/house-rules.md`), and cupertino literally claims the generic trigger "check this against our standards". |
| `cupertino-handbook-draft` / `lehre-codify` | add-negative-trigger | — (prompts split) | Descriptive per-dimension prose handbook vs prescriptive authority-cited `.lehre/ruleset.json`; neither description says which side of descriptive-vs-prescriptive it sits on. |
| `confab-contract-drift` / `self-assess-docs-drift` | add-negative-trigger | self-assess-docs-drift | The word "docstrings" reads as "docs" to a router, and confab's prose-exclusion rule lives only in the body the router never sees. |
| `lehre-codify` / `self-assess-extract-rules` | add-negative-trigger | — (underspecified) | How code is written vs what the code decides; both descriptions are purely positive, so the generic word "rules" is all a router has. |
| `consistency-map` / `consistency-scan` | add-negative-trigger | consistency-scan | Consecutive pipeline stages: map renders an inventory scan must already have produced, but its description states no precondition. |
| `compass-map-relationships` / `self-assess-stage-map` | add-negative-trigger | self-assess-stage-map | A domain-agnostic traversal technique whose unqualified "dependency path" phrasing catches repo import-graph prompts stage-map owns verbatim. |
| `self-assess-arch-health` / `self-assess-stage-map` | intended-handoff | self-assess-stage-map | arch-health's description states it reads `stage_graph.json`; Step 0 hard-gates on it and degrades to Ready-with-gaps. Firing both in order is correct. |
| `andon-verify` / `self-assess-transform-execute` | intended-handoff | self-assess-transform-execute | transform-execute's `verify-dispatch-handoff` rule forbids self-verifying and dispatches andon-verify itself. |
| `andon-loop` / `andon-verify` | intended-handoff | andon-loop | andon-loop is the sole ledger writer; andon-verify's own body says "never write to the ledger — andon-loop persists it". |
| `andon-loop` / `self-assess-transform-execute` | add-negative-trigger | andon-loop | Repo-wide iterative ledger pass vs exactly one authorized `MODERNIZATION_BRIEF.md` phase; neither description names the other's artifact. |
| `cupertino-handbook-fix` / `self-assess-idiom-fix` | add-negative-trigger | cupertino-handbook-fix | Both self-describe as "the mechanical-subset applier"; disjoint summary JSONs and mode gates, but neither says which artifact it does *not* consume. |
| `andon-verify` / `confab-assertion-audit` | add-negative-trigger | andon-verify | confab's trailing enforcement clause "Verification is mandatory and cannot be skipped by any setting" is the only landing spot for a generic verify-this-adversarially prompt. |
| `cupertino-council` / `self-assess-ui-audit` | add-negative-trigger | self-assess-ui-audit | Generative pre-code vs static pass over existing UI; "before we ship" places the prompt after code exists, and neither description excludes the other. |
| `confab-status` / `self-assess-status` | leave | — | Disjoint artifact sets, both descriptions already lead with their plugin name; the prompt names no pipeline. |
| `andon-status` / `self-assess-status` | leave | — | Same; cross-referencing would grow N² across 4+ status siblings and still not disambiguate a bare "status" prompt. |
| `andon-status` / `confab-status` | leave | — | Same; both are read-only and required to say "never run" rather than fabricate, so a misroute costs a sentence. |
| `confab-preflight` / `self-assess-preflight` | leave | — | Both correctly plugin-scoped on disk; "the pipeline" is a definite reference with no antecedent. |
| `andon-preflight` / `self-assess-preflight` | rescope | — | Asymmetric defect: andon scopes every trigger by name while self-assess claims the bare literal "run preflight", which four preflights have equal claim to. |
| `andon-preflight` / `confab-preflight` | leave | — | Family-wide ambiguity, not pairwise; a mutual clause fixes 1 of ~20 pairs. |
| `cli-scaffold-compiled` / `scaffold-cli` | intended-handoff | scaffold-cli | Declared bidirectional dispatch edge; scaffold-cli owns language resolution in code, which direct paradigm dispatch skips. |
| `andon-challenger` / `consistency-critic` | leave | — | Same genus (adversarial read-only review), different objects (wire contract vs Pattern Card); the probe prompt names no object. |
| `handbook-remediator` / `idiom-remediator` | add-negative-trigger | — (neither should fire) | Shared contract *shape*, different finding streams; neither says it is dispatch-only. |
| `conformance-remediator` / `handbook-remediator` | add-negative-trigger | — (neither should fire) | Verbatim-identical abstract job description ("apply one already-verified mechanical fix at the cited location and nothing else") with no domain named. |
| `conformance-remediator` / `idiom-remediator` | add-negative-trigger | — (neither should fire) | Same; different upstream contracts, different verifiers (lehre-validate vs andon-verify), no discriminator in either description. |

Thirteen pairs resolve by appending negative triggers, four are intended handoffs that need no change, six are prompt-side ambiguity, one is a rescope. No pair produced a merge verdict — no two components in the workshop do the same job.

Nit (rubric F6): `codebase-consistency` ships its user-facing surface as commands (`plugins/codebase-consistency/commands/consistency-{scan,map,canonize,brief,align,verify,preflight,status}.md`) rather than skills, unlike the other eight plugins; the one intra-plugin collision above is therefore between two command descriptions.

### Structural duplication

**Remediator agents — three implementations of one contract.** `plugins/cupertino/agents/handbook-remediator.md`, `plugins/self-assess/agents/idiom-remediator.md` and `plugins/lehre/agents/conformance-remediator.md` collide pairwise in all three combinations, and every judgement found the same cause: an identical abstract job description with no domain and no dispatch-only clause. *Recommendation:* keep all three — the upstream finding shapes, mode gates and verifiers are genuinely disjoint — and apply the reciprocal negative triggers plus an explicit "only ever dispatched by `<orchestrator>`" clause already specified in the cupertino, lehre and self-assess backlogs.

**Fix skills.** `cupertino-handbook-fix` and `self-assess-idiom-fix` both self-describe as the mechanical-subset applier that refuses the judgment-requiring rest. *Recommendation:* reciprocal negative triggers naming the consuming artifact (`.cupertino/handbook_check_<domain>_summary.json` vs `code_idiom_summary.json` modernization rows), and correct `docs/orchestration/README.md`'s "mechanical rewrites" phrasing for self-assess-idiom-fix, which the judgement identifies as the upstream source of the shared word.

**Adversarial critics.** `plugins/andon/agents/andon-challenger.md` and `plugins/codebase-consistency/agents/consistency-critic.md` share the genus but not the object. *Recommendation:* leave both unchanged — the judgement is explicit that a cross-reference would be noise, since a router that cannot pick on this prompt has nothing on which to act on a pointer either.

**Status and preflight families.** Four status skills (`andon-status`, `confab-status`, `self-assess-status`, plus `/consistency-status`) and four preflights (`andon-preflight`, `confab-preflight`, `lehre-preflight`, `self-assess-preflight`) produced six `leave` verdicts and one `rescope`. *Recommendation:* do not cross-reference — the ambiguity is family-wide and the clause list would grow N² and rot on the next plugin, the same drift shape `lint-release-wiring.py` exists to catch. Do apply the one asymmetric fix: `plugins/self-assess/skills/self-assess-preflight/SKILL.md` must drop the unqualified literal trigger "run preflight" (opus-tier, the only trigger-set rewrite in the corpus), and resolve unqualified prompts at dispatch time by which pipeline's artifacts exist on disk.

**The duplicated reference file.** `references/parallel-safe-research-protocol.md` is byte-identical across `andon`, `compass`, `confab`, `cupertino` and `self-assess` (lint's only duplicate cluster). The cupertino backlog names the canonical source — `tools/symbol-indexer/parallel-safe-research-protocol.md`, synced by `rrt artifacts --regenerate` — while the andon backlog files the same fact as an undecided nit. *Recommendation:* record the sync relationship once so the lint result stops reading as a defect, and edit only the canonical file (the compass, cupertino and self-assess backlogs each carry a pending edit to this text — all three must land upstream, not in a plugin copy).

### Backlog by model tier

183 backlog entries across nine plugins.

| Tier | Entries | Kinds of fix | Plugins dominating |
|---|---|---|---|
| haiku | 44 | Negative-trigger and precondition clause insertions into descriptions; term renames (`hits`→`violations`, `commodity function`→`commodity feature`, `[convention-pattern]`→`[dimension-pattern]`); frontmatter shape conversions (`M-AGENT-TOOLS-SHAPE`, `M-DESC-PERSON`); pure tool-grant deletions; two `solve.js` one-line variable-source corrections; TOC and count-word fixes | compass 7, cupertino 7, lehre 6, self-assess 6, codebase-consistency 5, confab 5 |
| sonnet | 132 | The bulk: missing gate failure paths, absent-input branches, unbounded retry loops, undeclared inputs, schema/contract reconciliation, and every `Q-PROC-OUTPUT-SHOWN` fenced worked instance | compass 24, cupertino 21, self-assess 21, lehre 18, codebase-consistency 17, andon 13, cli-scaffold 9, confab 9 |
| opus | 2 | Scope-narrowing rewrites and unresolved design: `self-assess-preflight`'s trigger-set rescope, and a real `output_contract` gate for `plugins/compass/workflows/solve.js`'s Execute wave loop | self-assess 1, compass 1 |
| human | 5 | Branch decisions a model must not guess: `andon-loop`'s stage-or-gap filter (delete vs wire); `consistency-brief.md`'s pilot ordering semantics; `compass-map-relationships`'s pipeline claim (drop the clause vs add a fifth stage mode); `self-assess-extract-rules`'s vestigial-vs-intended `DATA_OBJECTS.md`/`business_rules.json` artifacts; whether the five-way reference duplication is intentional | andon 2 |

Severity skews the same way as tier: exactly one **blocker** in the corpus (`plugins/cupertino/workflows/handbook-fix.js`), and every plugin's verdict is `needs-work` with the summaries agreeing that no component needs redesign or merging.

### Ten actions

1. Gate `plugins/cupertino/workflows/handbook-fix.js`'s incoming `findings` before clustering — the corpus's only blocker, where a missing arg is indistinguishable from zero findings and unvalidated `.rule` yields `RULE: undefined` clusters.
2. Wire the dead injection-reporting contract in `plugins/codebase-consistency/workflows/{scan,verify,canonize}.js`, replacing `verify.js:145`'s `for (const s of [])` — the exact code-that-silently-does-nothing shape this repo's own CLAUDE.md catalogues.
3. Close the lehre human-approval hole end to end: define `approved_phases` in `plugins/lehre/skills/lehre-brief/SKILL.md`, read it in `lehre-conform`, and add the missing brief-approval refusal to `plugins/lehre/agents/conformance-remediator.md`.
4. Read `priorClarify`/`priorExplore`/`requestedBranches`/`maxBranchCount` from `parsedArgs` and move `phasesRun.push('Revise')` inside the criteria gate in `plugins/compass/workflows/solve.js` — two haiku edits that stop prior-run reuse vanishing and stop the contract claiming a phase that never ran.
5. Make cupertino's gating state flags clear on every path in `plugins/cupertino/skills/{cupertino-handbook-check,cupertino-handbook-fix,cupertino-review}/SKILL.md`, including a failure gate before review's flag cleanup discards the only copy of stage content.
6. Apply the thirteen negative-trigger insertions in one pass across the descriptions named in the collisions table (eleven haiku, two sonnet) — the cheapest fix in the corpus and the one that resolves every measured non-handoff collision.
7. Correct the two false contracts in `plugins/andon/references/okf-ledger-schema.md` (the `ANDON_BOARD.md` output_dir claim, and the undocumented source of the `lane:fast|slow` tag).
8. Fix tool grants that contradict stated roles: drop `Bash` from the five read-only self-assess auditors, drop `Glob` from `plugins/cupertino/agents/handbook-drift-auditor.md`, close `plugins/self-assess/agents/transform-executor.md`'s delete/move gap via a `files_to_delete` output field, and rewrite `plugins/lehre/agents/pattern-investigator.md`'s serena paragraph to match its actual `Read, Glob, Grep, Bash` grant.
9. Make `plugins/andon/skills/andon-preflight/SKILL.md` honest about the two files Step 3 writes, and gate the JSON write the way the structurally identical markdown write already is.
10. Route the five human-tier decisions to a maintainer before any automated pass runs — `andon-loop`'s filter argument, `consistency-brief.md`'s pilot semantics, `compass-map-relationships`'s pipeline claim, `self-assess-extract-rules`'s unclaimed artifacts, and the reference-file duplication — since each has two defensible branches and a guessed one encodes the wrong contract downstream.

### Instrument

Calibration ran twice and improved on both axes that matter — recall 0.76 → 0.80 with the worst per-angle recall rising from 0.40 to 0.60, at the cost of one false positive appearing in round 2 (zero in round 1) — and the sealed-set recall of 0.786 sits between the two calibration rounds, so the instrument behaved on unseen material roughly as it did on the calibration set. The pipeline raised 464 raw findings, of which 216 survived verification and 28 were extras beyond the seeded set, meaning slightly over half of what the finders proposed did not survive a second look; the backlogs above contain only the verified 216, and the 0.786 sealed recall means roughly one finding in five of the kind this instrument looks for was missed. Read this report as a high-precision, incomplete inventory: what it lists is checked, and a plugin's absence from a section is not evidence of its health — with a per-angle floor of 0.60, any single angle's silence is the weakest signal in the report.
